III.1.2
The
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\,}_{x}
phase

The
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\,}_{x}
phase is the only one of the four gapped phases
for which it is possible to find a ground state
|\Psi\rangle
such that,
in the thermodynamic limit,

\overline{G^{2,x}}[\Psi]\neq 0,\qquad\overline{G^{2,y}}[\Psi]=0,\qquad\overline{G^{4,x}}[\Psi]=0,\qquad\overline{G^{4,y}}[\Psi]=0,

(48)

in Fig.
7
.

With PBC, any one of the four corners in Fig.
5

is unitarily equivalent to a classical antiferromagnetic
nearest-neighbor Ising chain with the Hamiltonian

\widehat{H}^{\,}_{0}(0,0)
defined in Eq. (
17a
).

For the classical antiferromagnetic nearest-neighbor Ising chain

\widehat{H}^{\,}_{0}(0,0)
, all ordered energy eigenvalues

E^{\,}_{0}\leq E^{\,}_{1}\leq\cdots\leq E^{\,}_{n}\leq E^{\,}_{n+1}\leq\cdots

(49)

with the orthonormal eigenfunctions

|\Psi^{\,}_{0}\rangle,\ |\Psi^{\,}_{1}\rangle,\ \cdots,\ |\Psi^{\,}_{n}\rangle,\ |\Psi^{\,}_{n+1}\rangle,\ \cdots

(50)

are at least two-fold degenerate, for any spin configuration must share the
same energy as the one obtained by reversing the direction of all
spins through the operation of time reversal
\widehat{T}
.
Moreover, any two distinct but consecutive energy eigenvalues
are equally spaced.
This spacing is the gap
\Delta^{\,}_{0}(0,0)
that can be
computed from subtracting the energy of any one of the two

\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\,}_{x}
states

4
4

4

The state
\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}

is the representation
in the basis that diagonalizes

{\widehat{\sigma}^{x}_{j}}

of the state
\ket*{\mathrm{\hbox{N\'eel}}^{\,}_{x},+}

defined by Eq. (
17c
)
in the basis that diagonalizes

{\widehat{\sigma}^{z}_{j}}

\displaystyle\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}\coloneqq|\leftarrow\rightarrow\cdots\leftarrow\rightarrow\ \leftarrow\rightarrow\ \leftarrow\rightarrow\cdots\leftarrow\rightarrow\rangle,

(51a)

\displaystyle\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{1}}\coloneqq\widehat{T}\,\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{0}},\qquad\widehat{T}\,|\rightarrow\rangle=\mathrm{i}|\leftarrow\rangle,\quad\widehat{T}\,|\leftarrow\rangle=-\mathrm{i}|\rightarrow\rangle,

(51b)

in the basis for which

\displaystyle\widehat{\sigma}^{x}_{j}\,|\rightarrow\rangle^{\,}_{j}=+|\rightarrow\rangle^{\,}_{j},

(51c)

\displaystyle\widehat{\sigma}^{x}_{j}\,|\leftarrow\rangle^{\,}_{j}=-|\leftarrow\rangle^{\,}_{j},

(51d)

from the energy of one of the excited states, say

\displaystyle\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{2}}\coloneqq\begin{cases}|\leftarrow\rightarrow\cdots\leftarrow\rightarrow\ \leftarrow{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\!\!\rightarrow}}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rightarrow\!\!}}\leftarrow\cdots\rightarrow\leftarrow\rangle,&\hbox{ for OBC,}\\
&\\
|\leftarrow\rightarrow\cdots\leftarrow\!{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\!\rightarrow}}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rightarrow}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\leftarrow}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\leftarrow\!\!}}\rightarrow\cdots\leftarrow\rightarrow\rangle,&\hbox{ for PBC,}\end{cases}

(51e)

\displaystyle\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{3}}\coloneqq\widehat{T}\,\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{2}},

(51f)

The existence of the energy gap
E^{\,}_{2}-E^{\,}_{1}

guarantees the stability of
the
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\,}_{x}
phase in the phase diagram in Fig.
5
.

The energy with respect of Hamiltonian
\widehat{H}^{\,}_{1}(0,0)

of the
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\,}_{x}
state
(
51a
)
and its time-reversed counterpart
(
51b
)
is that of the twofold-degenerate
ground-state energy

E^{\,}_{0}=E^{\,}_{1}

of
\widehat{H}^{\,}_{1}(0,0)
.
Under OBC, this energy is
[see Eq. (
36
)]

E^{\,}_{0}=-2N+1

(52a)

for
\widehat{H}^{\,}_{1}(0,0)

and it is the reference energy in
Fig.
8
.
Under PBC, this energy is
[see Eq. (
35
)]

E^{\,}_{0}=-2N

(52b)

for
\widehat{H}^{\,}_{0}(0,0)

and it is the reference energy in
Fig.
9
.
The first excited state defined in Eq. (
51e
)
of Hamiltonian
\widehat{H}^{\,}_{1}(0,0)

is
[see Eq. (
36
)]

E^{\,}_{2}=-2N+3

(53a)

when OBC are chosen.
It follows that the differences in the energies

E^{\,}_{1}-E^{\,}_{0}
and
E^{\,}_{2}-E^{\,}_{1}

of Fig.
8

are independent of
2N
.
The first excited state defined in Eq. (
51e
)
of Hamiltonian
\widehat{H}^{\,}_{0}(0,0)

is
[see Eq. (
35
)]

E^{\,}_{2}=-2N+4

(53b)

when PBC are chosen.
Hereto, it follows that the differences in the energies

E^{\,}_{1}-E^{\,}_{0}
and
E^{\,}_{2}-E^{\,}_{1}

of Fig.
9

are independent of
2N
.
The dependence on the choice between OBC and PBC
for the value of
E^{\,}_{2}-E^{\,}_{1}

can be understood as follows.
Under OBC, excitations can support an odd number of domain walls, e.g.,
Eq. (
51e
),
with each domain wall contributing the
energy
+2
, e.g., Fig.
8
.
In contrast,
under PBC, excitations must support an even number of domain walls,
e.g.,
Eq. (
51e
),
with each domain wall
contributing an energy of
+2
, e.g.,
Fig.
9
.
This difference in the structure of the excitations
affects the degeneracy of the excitation energy
E^{\,}_{2}
.
For OBC, the degeneracy for the lowest lying excited states is

5
5

5

With OBC, select either one of the two ground states

|\Psi^{\,}_{0}\rangle

or

|\Psi^{\,}_{1}\rangle

by fixing the eigenvalue of
{\widehat{X}^{\,}_{1}}

on the first site. There are
“choose one sites out of
2N-1
sites” ways
of creating one domain wall labeled by
j=2,\cdots,2N

by reversing the eigenvalues of

{\widehat{X}^{\,}_{k}}

for all
k=j,j+1,\cdots,2N
.
This is the degeneracy
d^{(1)}_{1}

defined in
Eq. (
36b
)

2(2N-1).

(54a)

For PBC, the degeneracy for the lowest lying excited states is

6
6

6

With PBC, starting from either the classical antiferromagnetic states

|\Psi^{\,}_{0}\rangle

or

|\Psi^{\,}_{1}\rangle
,
there are
“choose two sites out of
2N
sites” ways
of creating two domain walls
labeled by
j=1,\cdots,2N
and
j^{\prime}=j+1,\cdots,2N

by reversing the eigenvalues of

{\widehat{X}^{\,}_{k}}
with
j\leq k\leq j^{\prime}
.
The same degeneracy
2N(2N-1)
is obtained from observing that

E^{(0)}_{n}=E^{(0)}_{n+1}
for
n=1,3,5,\cdots,2N-5,2N-3
in
Eq. (
35a
)
to derive the number of ways there are to insert two domain walls by
adding
d^{(0)}_{2}
to
d^{(0)}_{1}
defined in
Eq. (
35b
)

2N(2N-1).

(54b)

These distinctions highlight the significant impact
of boundary conditions on the energy eigenvalue spectrum and the corresponding
physical properties of the
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\,}_{x}
phase.

When moving slightly away from the corner
(0,0)
in Fig.
5
,
the
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\,}_{x}
state
(
51a
)
and its time-reversed counterpart
(
51b
)
are no longer exact ground states.
Moreover, for any finite number
2N
of sites, the energy
E^{\,}_{0}

is strictly lower than the energy
E^{\,}_{1}
of the first excited state.
However, the energy spacing
E^{\,}_{1}-E^{\,}_{0}

between the ground state energy
E^{\,}_{0}
and the first
excited state energy
E^{\,}_{1}

is observed numerically to vanish exponentially fast with
2N

so that a two-fold degeneracy of the ground state is recovered
in the thermodynamic limit
2N\to\infty
.
For example, for a chain made of
2N=32
sites at
(\pi/20,1/10)
,

E^{\,}_{1}-E^{\,}_{0}\approx 0

within numerical precision under both PBC and OBC.

We have tracked the excitation energy
E^{\,}_{2}
,
which remains non-degenerate with
E^{\,}_{0}
in the thermodynamic limit

2N\to\infty
, upon approaching the phase boundary to the

\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\,}_{x}
phase. The energy gap
E^{\,}_{2}-E^{\,}_{1}
at
(\pi/20,1/10)

is smaller than the energy gap
E^{\,}_{2}-E^{\,}_{1}
at
(0,0)

for any system size
2N
.
This difference is evident when comparing Figs.
8

and
8
for OBC and in
Figs.
9

and
9
for PBC.
This difference aligns with the
expectation of a gap closing upon exiting the
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\,}_{x}
phase.

At the corner
(0,0)
,
the two orthogonal ground states

|\Psi^{\,}_{0}\rangle

and

|\Psi^{\,}_{1}\rangle

of Hamiltonian
(
7
)
exhibit the staggered magnetization

|m^{x}_{\mathrm{sta}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]|=|m^{x}_{\mathrm{sta}}[\Psi^{\,}_{1}]|=1,

(55a)

indicating perfect classical (
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\,}_{x}
) antiferromagnetic order.
This is shown in
Fig.
10

for OBC and in
Fig.
11

for PBC.
More specifically, the ground
state
\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}
in Eq. (
51a
)
has the staggered magnetization

m^{x}_{\mathrm{sta}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]=+1,

(55b)

while its time-reversed image

\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{1}}
in Eq. (
51b
)
has the staggered magnetization

m^{x}_{\mathrm{sta}}[\Psi^{\,}_{1}]=-1.

(55c)

The states
\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}

in Eq. (
51a
)
and
\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{1}}

in Eq. (
51b
)
define an orthonormal basis for the ground states
of the Hamiltonian
\widehat{H}^{\,}_{0}(0,0)
.
Another basis of this two-dimensional
subspace associated with the eigenvalues
E^{\,}_{0}=E^{\,}_{1}
is
formed by the cat
states
\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{+}}
and
\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{-}}

defined by

\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{\pm}}\coloneqq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}\pm\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{1}}\right)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\widehat{\mathbb{1}}\pm\widehat{T}\right)\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}.

(56)

It follows that both states
\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{\pm}}

obey (here we make use of the hypothesis that
2N
is even to deduce that

\widehat{T}^{2}=\widehat{\mathbb{1}}
)

\widehat{T}\,\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{\pm}}\coloneqq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\widehat{T}\pm\widehat{T}^{2}\right)\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\widehat{T}\pm\widehat{\mathbb{1}}\right)\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}=\pm\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{\pm}}.

(57)

As
\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{\pm}}
are symmetric and antisymmetric linear combinations
of orthonormal eigenstates of opposite staggered magnetization

m^{x}_{\mathrm{sta}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]=-m^{x}_{\mathrm{sta}}[\Psi^{\,}_{1}],

(58)

the staggered magnetization of
\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{\pm}}

vanishes for any finite system size
2N
, i.e.,

m^{x}_{\mathrm{sta}}[\Psi^{\,}_{\pm}]=0.

(59)

For any finite number of sites
2N
,
it is only at the corner
(0,0)
of the

\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\,}_{x}
phase
that it is possible to choose a ground state with
non-vanishing magnetization,
for it is only there in the
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\,}_{x}
phase
that the ground state is exactly two-fold degenerate.
For any finite number of sites
2N
and at any point in the

\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\,}_{x}
phase distinct from the corner
(0,0)
,
the ground state
|\Psi^{\,}_{0}\rangle
is
a non-degenerate eigenstate of
\widehat{T}
,
and thus exhibits

m^{x}_{\mathrm{sta}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]=0
.
This prevents a priori the use of

m^{x}_{\mathrm{sta}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

as a probe to locate the phase transition upon exiting the

\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\,}_{x}

phase.

In DMRG, the initial state is chosen such that it is not an
eigenstate of the time-reversal operator
\widehat{T}
.
Consequently, the DMRG state
|\Psi^{\mathrm{DMRG}}_{0}\rangle

that approximates the energy of the true ground state
|\Psi^{\,}_{0}\rangle

to exponential accuracy can exhibit a non-vanishing value for

m^{x}_{\mathrm{sta}}[\Psi^{\mathrm{DMRG}}_{0}]

and can be used as a probe to survey the extend of the

\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\,}_{x}
phase. In effect, DMRG mimics the standard procedure
in statistical physics to detect spontaneous symmetry breaking
by which (i) the order parameter is coupled linearly to a source that is
then added to the Hamiltonian in order the break explicitly
the symmetry that is broken spontaneously by the order parameter acquiring
a non-vanishing expectation value,
(ii) the thermodynamic limit is taken, and
(iii) the source is switched off, i.e.,

m^{x}_{\mathrm{sta}}\coloneqq\lim_{B\to 0}\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\lim_{2N\to\infty}\,\frac{1}{2N}\sum_{j=1}^{2N}(-1)^{j}\expectationvalue*{\widehat{\sigma}^{x}_{j}}^{\,}_{\beta,B},

(60a)

where

\expectationvalue*{\widehat{\sigma}^{x}_{j}}^{\,}_{\beta,B}\coloneqq\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\,\left\{e^{-\beta\left[\widehat{H}^{\,}_{b}(\theta,\lambda)-B\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2N}(-1)^{j}\,\widehat{\sigma}^{x}_{j}\right]}\,\widehat{\sigma}^{x}_{j}\right\}}{\mathrm{Tr}\,\left\{e^{-\beta\left[\widehat{H}^{\,}_{b}(\theta,\lambda)-B\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2N}(-1)^{j}\,\widehat{\sigma}^{x}_{j}\right]}\hphantom{\,\widehat{\sigma}^{x}_{j}}\right\}}.

(60b)

Henceforth, we will use the symbol
\Psi^{\,}_{0}
as a reference to
the DMRG state that approximates the exact target eigenstate
in the thermodynamic limit that breaks spontaneously time-reversal symmetry.

at

.

As stated earlier,
when moving away from the corner
(0,0)

in Fig.

,
the
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\,}_{x}

(

)

(

)

, for instance at

) remains qualitatively similar to the

|\Psi^{\,}_{0}\rangle

to the ground state
at

from Fig.

|m^{x}_{\mathrm{sta}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]|\simeq 0.99<1,

(61)

and PBC in Fig.

,
relative to the classical value of one
at
(0,0)
from Fig.
5
, shown in Fig.

for OBC and Fig.

for PBC.
This reduction from
the classical (
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\,}_{x}
) value
(

)
to

|m^{x}_{\mathrm{sta}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]|\simeq 0.99

reflects the effects of quantum fluctuations that arises
as the first line on the right-hand side of
Eq. (
7a
)
does not commute with the second line.

At the corner
(0,0)
,
the excited state
|\Psi^{\,}_{2}\rangle
with the energy
E^{\,}_{2}

in Eq. (

)
supports one (two) domain wall(s) for OBC (PBC).
The existence of the domain walls shows up in the
dependence on
j
of the local expectation value

\langle\widehat{X}^{\,}_{j}\rangle^{\,}_{\Psi^{\,}_{2}}\equiv\langle\widehat{\sigma}^{x}_{j}\rangle^{\,}_{\Psi^{\,}_{2}}\coloneqq\langle\Psi^{\,}_{2}|\,\widehat{\sigma}^{x}_{j}\,|\Psi^{\,}_{2}\rangle.

(62)

Whereas Fig.

shows the dependence
(-1)^{j}
on

for

,

and

when OBC are selected.

(-1)^{j}
on

for

,
Fig.

and

and one located between

and

when PBC are selected.

from Fig.

,

is
0.99\times(-1)^{j+1}
for OBC in Fig.

and
0.99\times(-1)^{j+1}
for PBC in Fig.

.
At
(\pi/20,1/10)
from Fig.

,
the difference between OBC and PBC for

\cos\big(\pi j/2N\big)

,

.
For PBC,

.

III.1.3
The
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\,}_{y}
phase

The
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\,}_{y}
phase is the only one of the four gapped phases
for which it is possible to find a ground state
|\Psi\rangle
such that,
in the thermodynamic limit,

\overline{G^{2,x}}[\Psi]=0,\qquad\overline{G^{2,y}}[\Psi]\neq 0,\qquad\overline{G^{4,x}}[\Psi]=0,\qquad\overline{G^{4,y}}[\Psi]=0,

(63)

in Fig.
7
.

The unitary transformation
\widehat{U}^{\,}_{\mathrm{R}}

defined in Eq. (
12a
)
realizes the reflection about the line
\theta=\pi/4

from the phase diagram in Fig.
5

for both PBC and OBC, since

\widehat{U}^{\,}_{\mathrm{R}}\,\widehat{H}^{\,}_{b}(\theta,\lambda)\,\widehat{U}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{R}}=\widehat{H}^{\,}_{b}(\pi/2-\theta,\lambda)

(64)

holds for
b=0,1
.
Consequently, the numerical characterization
of the
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\,}_{x}
phase
made in Sec.
III.1.2
must also hold for
the
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\,}_{y}
phase if the replacements

(\theta,\lambda)\to(\pi/2-\theta,\lambda),\qquad\widehat{X}^{\,}_{j}\to\widehat{Y}^{\,}_{j},\qquad m^{x}_{\mathrm{sta}}\to m^{y}_{\mathrm{sta}},

(65)

are made. We have verified that both the ED and DMRG
simulations agree with this prediction.

III.1.4
The
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\mathrm{SPT}}_{x}
phase

The
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\mathrm{SPT}}_{x}
phase is the only one of the four gapped phases
for which it is possible to find a ground state
|\Psi\rangle
such that,
in the thermodynamic limit,

\overline{G^{2,x}}[\Psi]=0,\qquad\overline{G^{2,y}}[\Psi]=0,\qquad\overline{G^{4,x}}[\Psi]=0,\qquad\overline{G^{4,y}}[\Psi]\neq 0,

(66)

in Fig.
7
.

The unitary transformation
\widehat{U}^{\,}_{\mathrm{E}}

defined in Eq. (
12b
)
realizes the reflection about the line
\lambda=1/2

from the phase diagram in Fig.
5

for PBC, since

\widehat{U}^{\,}_{\mathrm{E}}\,\widehat{H}^{\,}_{b=0}(\theta,\lambda)\,\widehat{U}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{E}}=\widehat{H}^{\,}_{b=0}(\theta,1/2-\lambda)

(67)

holds if and only if
b=0
.
The composition

\widehat{U}^{\,}_{\mathrm{CZ}}\equiv\widehat{U}^{\,}_{\mathrm{E}}\,\widehat{U}^{\,}_{\mathrm{R}}

realizes the rotation by
\pi
about the center
(\pi/4,1/2)

from the phase diagram in Fig.
5

for PBC, since

\widehat{U}^{\,}_{\mathrm{CZ}}\,\widehat{H}^{\,}_{b=0}(\theta,\lambda)\,\widehat{U}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{CZ}}=\widehat{H}^{\,}_{b=0}(\pi/2-\theta,1/2-\lambda)

(68)

holds if and only if
b=0
.
Hence, when PBC are imposed, the properties of
the
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\mathrm{SPT}}_{x}
phase
are unitarily equivalent
under conjugation by

\widehat{U}^{\,}_{\mathrm{CZ}}

to the ones of the
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\,}_{x}
phase.

After imposing PBC at the corner
(\pi/2,1)

from Fig.
5
,
we know from Sec.
II.1

that the ordered energy eigenvalues

E^{\,}_{0}\leq E^{\,}_{1}\leq\cdots\leq E^{\,}_{n}\leq E^{\,}_{n+1}\leq\cdots

(69)

with the orthonormal eigenfunctions

|\Psi^{\,}_{0}\rangle,\ |\Psi^{\,}_{1}\rangle,\ \cdots,\ |\Psi^{\,}_{n}\rangle,\ |\Psi^{\,}_{n+1}\rangle,\ \cdots

(70)

of Hamiltonian

\widehat{H}^{\,}_{0}(\pi/2,1)

defined in Eq. (
21b
),
are at least two-fold degenerate
for any number
2N
of sites.
Indeed, any “spin” configuration must share the
same energy as the one obtained by reversing the direction of all “spins”
through the operation of reversal of time
\widehat{T}
. Moreover,
any two distinct but consecutive energy eigenvalues are equally spaced.
This spacing is the gap

\Delta^{\,}_{0}(\pi/2,1)=\Delta^{\,}_{0}(0,0)

that can be computed from subtracting the energy of any one of the two

\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\mathrm{SPT}}_{x}
states

7
7

7

The state
\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}

is the image by
{\widehat{U}^{\,}_{\mathrm{CZ}}}

of the representation
in the basis that diagonalizes

{\widehat{\sigma}^{x}_{j}}

of the state
\ket*{\mathrm{\hbox{N\'eel}}^{\,}_{x},+}

defined by Eq. (
17c
)
in the basis that diagonalizes

{\widehat{\sigma}^{z}_{j}}

\displaystyle\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}\coloneqq|\Leftarrow\Rightarrow\cdots\Leftarrow\Rightarrow\ \Leftarrow\Rightarrow\ \Leftarrow\Rightarrow\cdots\Leftarrow\Rightarrow\rangle,

(71a)

\displaystyle\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{1}}\coloneqq\widehat{T}\,\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{0}},\qquad\widehat{T}\,|\Rightarrow\rangle^{\,}_{j}=\mathrm{i}|\Leftarrow\rangle^{\,}_{j},\quad\widehat{T}\,|\Rightarrow\rangle^{\,}_{j}=-\mathrm{i}|\Leftarrow\rangle^{\,}_{j},

(71b)

in the “spin” basis for which

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{\sigma}^{z}_{j-1}\,\widehat{\sigma}^{x}_{j}\,\widehat{\sigma}^{z}_{j+1}\right)|\Rightarrow\rangle^{\,}_{j}=+|\Rightarrow\rangle^{\,}_{j},\qquad\widehat{\sigma}^{z}_{j-1}\,\widehat{\sigma}^{x}_{j}\,\widehat{\sigma}^{z}_{j+1}=\widehat{U}^{\,}_{\mathrm{CZ}}\,\widehat{\sigma}^{x}_{j}\,\widehat{U}^{{\dagger}}_{\mathrm{CZ}},\qquad|\Rightarrow\rangle^{\,}_{j}=\widehat{U}^{\,}_{\mathrm{CZ}}\,|\rightarrow\rangle,

(71c)

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{\sigma}^{z}_{j-1}\,\widehat{\sigma}^{x}_{j}\,\widehat{\sigma}^{z}_{j+1}\right)|\Leftarrow\rangle^{\,}_{j}=-|\Leftarrow\rangle^{\,}_{j},\qquad\widehat{\sigma}^{z}_{j-1}\,\widehat{\sigma}^{x}_{j}\,\widehat{\sigma}^{z}_{j+1}=\widehat{U}^{\,}_{\mathrm{CZ}}\,\widehat{\sigma}^{x}_{j}\,\widehat{U}^{{\dagger}}_{\mathrm{CZ}},\qquad|\Leftarrow\rangle^{\,}_{j}=\widehat{U}^{\,}_{\mathrm{CZ}}\,|\leftarrow\rangle,

(71d)

from the energy of one of the excited states, say

\displaystyle\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{2}}\coloneqq|\Leftarrow\Rightarrow\cdots\Leftarrow\!{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\!\Rightarrow}}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Rightarrow}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Leftarrow}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Leftarrow\!\!}}\Rightarrow\cdots\Leftarrow\Rightarrow\rangle,

(71e)

\displaystyle\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{3}}\coloneqq\widehat{T}\,\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{2}},\qquad\widehat{T}\,|\Rightarrow\rangle^{\,}_{j}=\mathrm{i}|\Leftarrow\rangle^{\,}_{j},\quad\widehat{T}\,|\Rightarrow\rangle^{\,}_{j}=-\mathrm{i}|\Leftarrow\rangle^{\,}_{j},

(71f)

The existence of this gap guarantees the stability of
the
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\mathrm{SPT}}_{x}
phase in the phase diagram in Fig.
5
.

(

.

In contrast, after imposing OBC at the corner
(\pi/2,1)

from Fig.
5
,
we know from Sec.
II.1

that the ground states of
Hamiltonian
\widehat{H}^{\,}_{1}(\pi/2,1)

defined in Eq. (
21b
)
are eightfold degenerate for any number
2N
of sites.
An orthonormal basis for these ground states is

\displaystyle\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}\coloneqq|\uparrow\,\Leftarrow\Rightarrow\cdots\Leftarrow\Rightarrow\cdots\Leftarrow\Rightarrow\,\uparrow\rangle,

(72a)

\displaystyle\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{1}}\coloneqq\widehat{T}\,\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{0}},

(72b)

\displaystyle\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{2}}\coloneqq|\uparrow\,\Leftarrow\Rightarrow\cdots\Leftarrow\Rightarrow\cdots\Leftarrow\Rightarrow\,\downarrow\rangle,

(72c)

\displaystyle\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{3}}\coloneqq\widehat{T}\,\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{2}},

(72d)

\displaystyle\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{4}}\coloneqq|\downarrow\,\Leftarrow\Rightarrow\cdots\Leftarrow\Rightarrow\cdots\Leftarrow\Rightarrow\,\uparrow\rangle,

(72e)

\displaystyle\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{5}}\coloneqq\widehat{T}\,\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{4}},

(72f)

\displaystyle\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{6}}\coloneqq|\downarrow\,\Leftarrow\Rightarrow\cdots\Leftarrow\Rightarrow\cdots\Leftarrow\Rightarrow\,\downarrow\rangle,

(72g)

\displaystyle\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{7}}\coloneqq\widehat{T}\,\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{6}},

(72h)

where the state
\ket*{\uparrow}^{\,}_{j}
and
\ket*{\downarrow}^{\,}_{j}

are defined by the action

\displaystyle\widehat{\sigma}^{z}_{j}\,\ket*{\uparrow}^{\,}_{j}=+\ket*{\uparrow}^{\,}_{j},\qquad\widehat{T}\ket*{\uparrow}^{\,}_{j}=\mathrm{i}\ket*{\downarrow}^{\,}_{j},

(72i)

\displaystyle\widehat{\sigma}^{z}_{j}\,\ket*{\downarrow}^{\,}_{j}=-\ket*{\downarrow}^{\,}_{j},\qquad\widehat{T}\ket*{\downarrow}^{\,}_{j}=-\mathrm{i}\ket*{\uparrow}^{\,}_{j},

(72j)

for
j=1,\cdots,2N
.

When OBC are chosen,
the ground-state (first excited) energy of

\widehat{H}^{\,}_{1}(\pi/2,1)
is
[see Eq. (
36
)
and Fig.
12
]

E^{\,}_{0}=-2N+3\qquad(E^{\,}_{8}=-2N+5).

(73a)

When PBC are chosen,
the ground-state (first excited) energy of

\widehat{H}^{\,}_{0}(\pi/2,1)
is
[see Eq. (
35
)
and Fig.
13
]

E^{\,}_{0}=-2N\qquad(E^{\,}_{2}=-2N+4).

(73b)

When moving slightly away from the corner
(\pi/2,1)
in Fig.
5
under OBC,
the
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\mathrm{SPT}}_{x}
state (
71a
)
and its time-reversed counterpart (
71b
)
[the eight states (
72
)]
are no longer exact ground states.
Moreover, for any finite number
2N
of sites, the energy

E^{\,}_{0}

in Eq. (
73b
)
is strictly lower than the energy
E^{\,}_{1}

of the first excited state.
However, the energy spacing
E^{\,}_{1}-E^{\,}_{0}

between the ground-state energy
E^{\,}_{0}

in Eq. (
73b
)
and the first excited state energy
E^{\,}_{1}

is observed numerically to vanish exponentially fast with
2N

so that a two-fold degeneracy of the ground state is recovered
in the thermodynamic limit
2N\to\infty
.
For example, for a chain made of
2N=32
sites at
(19\pi/20,9/10)
,

E^{\,}_{1}-E^{\,}_{0}\approx 0

within numerical precision under both PBC and OBC.

We have tracked the excitation energy

E^{\,}_{8}

(
E^{\,}_{2}
)
in Eq. (
73a
)
[Eq. (
73b
)].
It remains non-degenerate with
E^{\,}_{0}
in the thermodynamic limit

2N\to\infty
upon approaching the phase boundary of the

\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\mathrm{SPT}}_{x}
phase. The energy gap

E^{\,}_{8}-E^{\,}_{7}

(
E^{\,}_{2}-E^{\,}_{1}
) at

(19\pi/20,9/10)

is smaller than the energy gap

E^{\,}_{8}-E^{\,}_{7}

(
E^{\,}_{2}-E^{\,}_{1}
)
at
(\pi/2,1)
for any system size
2N
.
This difference is evident when comparing
Figs.
12

and
12

for OBC
[Figs.
13

and
13

for PBC].
This difference aligns with the
expectation of a gap closing upon exiting the
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\mathrm{SPT}}_{x}
phase.

At the corner
(\pi/2,1)
from Fig.
5
,
the eight orthogonal ground states

\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{n}}
,
n=0,\cdots,7
, defined in
Eq. (
72
)
exhibit the staggered magnetization
[see Eq. (
44d
)]

|m^{x}_{\mathrm{sta,SPT}}[\Psi^{\,}_{n}]|=1,\qquad n=0,\cdots,7.

(74)

This saturation of the staggered magnetization
indicates perfect (
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\mathrm{SPT}}_{x}
) antiferromagnetic order
for the local order parameter

\widehat{U}^{\,}_{\mathrm{CZ}}\,\widehat{\sigma}^{x}_{j}\,\widehat{U}^{{\dagger}}_{\mathrm{CZ}}=\widehat{\sigma}^{z}_{j-1}\,\widehat{\sigma}^{x}_{j}\,\widehat{\sigma}^{z}_{j+1},\qquad j=2,\cdots,2N-1.

(75)

This is shown in Fig.
14
.
More specifically, four of
the ground states (
72
) have the staggered
magnetization

m^{x}_{\mathrm{sta,SPT}}[\Psi^{\,}_{n}]=-1,\qquad n=0,2,4,6,

(76)

while their time-reversed images have the staggered magnetization

m^{x}_{\mathrm{sta,SPT}}[\Psi^{\,}_{n}]=+1,\qquad n=1,3,5,7.

(77)

In fact, the four ground states

|\Psi^{\,}_{n}\rangle
with
n=0,1,2,4,6
(
n=1,2,5,7
)
from Eq. (
72
)
are eigenstates with eigenvalue

-1
(
+1
) of the staggered SPT ordered parameter

\widehat{m}^{x}_{\mathrm{sta,SPT}}\coloneqq\frac{1}{2N-2}\,\sum_{j=2}^{2N-1}(-1)^{j}\,\widehat{\sigma}^{z}_{j-1}\,\widehat{\sigma}^{x}_{j}\,\widehat{\sigma}^{z}_{j+1}.

(78)

The fourfold degeneracy of those ground states
that are eigenstates of
\widehat{m}^{x}_{\mathrm{sta,SPT}}

arises from the free quantum spin-
1/2

degrees of freedom at both ends the chain.
These free quantum spin-1/2
contribute to the expectation value of magnitude one for

\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{1}\equiv\widehat{\sigma}^{z}_{1}

and

\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{2N}\equiv\widehat{\sigma}^{z}_{2N}

as is shown in Fig.
14
.
In contrast, the two ground states
(
71
)
selected by PBC are non-degenerate eigenstates
of the staggered SPT ordered parameter

\widehat{m}^{x}_{\mathrm{sta,SPT}}\coloneqq\frac{1}{2N}\,\sum_{j=1}^{2N}(-1)^{j}\,\widehat{\sigma}^{z}_{j-1}\,\widehat{\sigma}^{x}_{j}\,\widehat{\sigma}^{z}_{j+1}=\widehat{U}^{\,}_{\mathrm{CZ}}\,\widehat{m}^{x}_{\mathrm{sta}}\,\widehat{U}^{{\dagger}}_{\mathrm{CZ}},\qquad\widehat{m}^{x}_{\mathrm{sta}}\coloneqq\frac{1}{2N}\,\sum_{j=1}^{2N}(-1)^{j}\,\widehat{\sigma}^{x}_{j}=-\widehat{T}\,\widehat{m}^{x}_{\mathrm{sta}}\,\widehat{T}.

(79)

This is shown in Fig.
15
.
As there are no free quantum spin-1/2 at the ends of the chains,
the expectation value of

\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j}\equiv\widehat{\sigma}^{z}_{j}

vanishes for all sites
j=1,\cdots,2N
,
as is shown in Fig.
15
.

.

Figure
14

for OBC
and
Fig.
15

for PBC show that,
at the corner
(\pi/2,1)
from Fig.
5
,
there are excited states

|\Psi\rangle

and sites
j

such that the expectation value

\langle\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j-1}\,\widehat{X}^{\,}_{j}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j+1}\rangle^{\,}_{\Psi}\equiv\langle\widehat{\sigma}^{z}_{j-1}\,\widehat{\sigma}^{x}_{j}\,\widehat{\sigma}^{z}_{j+1}\rangle^{\,}_{\Psi}=0

(80)

vanishes, while the expectation value

\left|\left\langle\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j}\right\rangle^{\,}_{\Psi}\right|\equiv\left|\left\langle\widehat{\sigma}^{z}_{j}\right\rangle^{\,}_{\Psi}\right|=1

(81)

saturates to its classical value, respectively.
This property is a signature of the extensive degeneracies
(
36b
)
for OBC
and
(
35b
)
for PBC, respectively,
of the excited states of the quantum spin-1/2 cluster Hamiltonians,
as we now explain.

Without loss of generality, we consider
the corner
(0,0)
from Fig.
5

under OBC. We choose the ground state

\ket*{\mathrm{\hbox{N\'eel}}^{\,}_{x}, +}\coloneqq|\leftarrow\rightarrow\cdots\leftarrow\rightarrow\ \leftarrow\rightarrow\ \leftarrow\rightarrow\cdots\ \leftarrow\rightarrow\ \rangle

(82a)

in the basis (
51c
) and (
51d
).
Its energy is
E^{\,}_{0}=-2N+1
[see Eq. (
52a
)].
Equipped with this ground state,
we define the pair of orthonormal excited eigenstates

\displaystyle\ket*{+^{\,}_{k}}\coloneqq\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{k}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{k+1}\,\cdots\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{2N-1}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{2N}\,\ket*{\mathrm{\hbox{N\'eel}}^{\,}_{x},+}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\ket*{+^{\,}_{k}}}=|\leftarrow\rightarrow\cdots\leftarrow{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\!\!\rightarrow}}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rightarrow\!\!}}\leftarrow\ \rightarrow\leftarrow\ \cdots\ \rightarrow\leftarrow\ \rangle,

(82b)

\displaystyle\ket*{+^{\,}_{k+1}}\coloneqq\widehat{\mathbb{1}}^{\,}_{k}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{k+1}\,\cdots\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{2N-1}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{2N}\,\ket*{\mathrm{\hbox{N\'eel}}^{\,}_{x},+}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\ket*{+^{\,}_{k+1}}}=|\leftarrow\rightarrow\ \cdots\ \leftarrow\rightarrow{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\leftarrow}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\leftarrow\!\!}}\ \rightarrow\leftarrow\ \cdots\ \rightarrow\leftarrow\ \rangle,

(82c)

for any
k=2,\cdots,2N
.
The excited energy eigenstate
\ket*{+^{\,}_{k}}

is a domain wall between the sites
k-1
and
k
.
The excited energy eigenstate
\ket*{+^{\,}_{k+1}}

is a domain wall between the sites
k
and
k+1
.
The pair of energy eigenstates
\ket*{+^{\,}_{k}}
and
\ket*{+^{\,}_{k+1}}

are degenerate with the energy eigenvalue
E^{\,}_{0}+2
.
Hence, the symmetric and antisymmetric linear combinations

\displaystyle\ket*{+^{(\mathrm{s})}_{k,k+1}}\coloneqq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\ket*{+^{\,}_{k}}+\ket*{+^{\,}_{k+1}}\right),

(83a)

\displaystyle\ket*{+^{(\mathrm{a})}_{k,k+1}}\coloneqq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\ket*{+^{\,}_{k}}-\ket*{+^{\,}_{k+1}}\right),

(83b)

are an orthonormal pair of excited energy eigenstates with
the energy eigenvalue
E^{\,}_{0}+2
.
The pair of identities

\displaystyle\ket*{+^{\,}_{k}}=\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{k}\ket*{+^{\,}_{k+1}},

(84a)

\displaystyle\ket*{+^{\,}_{k+1}}=\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{k}\ket*{+^{\,}_{k}},

(84b)

implies that

\displaystyle\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{k}\,\ket*{+^{(\mathrm{s})}_{k,k+1}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\widehat{Z}^{2}_{k}\,\ket*{+^{\,}_{k+1}}+\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{k}\,\ket*{+^{\,}_{k+1}}\right)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\ket*{+^{\,}_{k+1}}+\ket*{+^{\,}_{k}}\right)=+\ket*{+^{(\mathrm{s})}_{k,k+1}},

(85a)

\displaystyle\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{k}\,\ket*{+^{(\mathrm{a})}_{k,k+1}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\widehat{Z}^{2}_{k}\,\ket*{+^{\,}_{k+1}}-\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{k}\,\ket*{+^{\,}_{k+1}}\right)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\ket*{+^{\,}_{k+1}}-\ket*{+^{\,}_{k}}\right)=-\ket*{+^{(\mathrm{a})}_{k,k+1}}.

(85b)

In turn, the Pauli algebra obeyed by

\widehat{X}^{\,}_{j}
,

\widehat{Y}^{\,}_{j}
,
and

\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j}

implies that

\displaystyle\widehat{X}^{\,}_{k}\,\ket*{+^{(\mathrm{s})}_{k,k+1}}=\ket*{+^{(\mathrm{a})}_{k,k+1}},

(86a)

\displaystyle\widehat{X}^{\,}_{k}\,\ket*{+^{(\mathrm{a})}_{k,k+1}}=\ket*{+^{(\mathrm{s})}_{k,k+1}}.

(86b)

In particular, it follows that

\displaystyle\matrixelement*{+^{(\mathrm{s})}_{k,k+1}}{\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{k}\,}{+^{(\mathrm{s})}_{k,k+1}}=+1,

(87a)

\displaystyle\matrixelement*{+^{(\mathrm{a})}_{k,k+1}}{\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{k}\,}{+^{(\mathrm{a})}_{k,k+1}}=-1,

(87b)

while

\displaystyle\matrixelement*{+^{(\mathrm{s})}_{k,k+1}}{\,\widehat{X}^{\,}_{k}\,}{+^{(\mathrm{s})}_{k,k+1}}=\matrixelement*{+^{(\mathrm{a})}_{k,k+1}}{\,\widehat{X}^{\,}_{k}\,}{+^{(\mathrm{a})}_{k,k+1}}=0.

(87c)

On the other hand,
one verifies that, for any
l\neq k
,

8
8

8

The states

{\widehat{Z}}^{\,}_{l}\,|+^{\,}_{k}\rangle

and

{\widehat{Z}}^{\,}_{l}\,\,|+^{\,}_{k+1}\rangle

are orthogonal to both

|+^{\,}_{k}\rangle

and

|+^{\,}_{k+1}\rangle

if
l\neq k

\displaystyle\matrixelement*{+^{(\mathrm{s})}_{k,k+1}}{\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{l}\,}{+^{(\mathrm{s})}_{k,k+1}}=0,

(88a)

\displaystyle\matrixelement*{+^{(\mathrm{a})}_{k,k+1}}{\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{l}\,}{+^{(\mathrm{a})}_{k,k+1}}=0,

(88b)

while

9
9

9

After expressing the states

|+^{\mathrm{(s)}}_{k,k+1}\rangle

and

|+^{\mathrm{(a)}}_{k,k+1}\rangle

in terms of the state
\ket*{\mathrm{\hbox{N\'eel}}^{\,}_{x},+}
,
one may replace the operator

{\widehat{X}}^{\,}_{l}

by the
\mathbb{C}
-number

(-1)^{\delta^{\,}_{1,\mathrm{sgn}(l-k)}}\,(-1)^{l}

if
l\neq k

\displaystyle\matrixelement*{+^{(\mathrm{s})}_{k,k+1}}{\,\widehat{X}^{\,}_{l}\,}{+^{(\mathrm{s})}_{k,k+1}}=(-1)^{\delta^{\,}_{1,\mathrm{sgn}(l-k)}}\,(-1)^{l},

(88c)

\displaystyle\matrixelement*{+^{(\mathrm{a})}_{k,k+1}}{\,\widehat{X}^{\,}_{l}\,}{+^{(\mathrm{a})}_{k,k+1}}=(-1)^{\delta^{\,}_{1,\mathrm{sgn}(l-k)}}\,(-1)^{l}.

(88d)

We consider the corner
(\pi/2,1)
from Fig.
5
.
We choose the ground state

\ket*{\mathrm{\hbox{N\'eel}}^{\mathrm{SPT}}_{x}, -,\uparrow\downarrow}\coloneqq|\ \uparrow\ \Leftarrow\Rightarrow\cdots\Leftarrow\Rightarrow\ \Leftarrow\Rightarrow\ \Leftarrow\Rightarrow\cdots\ \Leftarrow\Rightarrow\ \downarrow\ \rangle

(89a)

when selecting OBC
in the basis
(
71c
)
and
(
71d
)
for sites
2,\cdots,2N-1

and in the basis that diagonalizes

\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{1}
and
\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{2N}

for sites 1 and
2N
.
We choose the ground state

\ket*{\mathrm{\hbox{N\'eel}}^{\mathrm{SPT}}_{x}, -}\coloneqq|\ \Rightarrow\ \Leftarrow\Rightarrow\cdots\Leftarrow\Rightarrow\ \Leftarrow\Rightarrow\ \Leftarrow\Rightarrow\cdots\ \Leftarrow\Rightarrow\ \Leftarrow\ \rangle

(89b)

when selecting PBC
in the basis
(
71c
)
and
(
71d
)
for all sites.
Equipped with the ground state (
89a
),
we define for any
k=2,\cdots,2N-1

the pair of orthonormal excited eigenstates

\displaystyle\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{8}}^{\,}_{k}\coloneqq|\,\uparrow\,\Leftarrow\Rightarrow\,\cdots\,\Leftarrow{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\!\!\Rightarrow}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Rightarrow\!\!}}\Leftarrow\,\Rightarrow\Leftarrow\,\cdots\,\Rightarrow\Leftarrow\,\downarrow\,\rangle\equiv\prod_{j=k}^{2N-1}\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j}\,\ket*{\mathrm{\hbox{N\'eel}}^{\mathrm{SPT}}_{x}, -,\uparrow\downarrow},

(90a)

\displaystyle\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{9}}^{\,}_{k+1}\coloneqq|\,\uparrow\,\Leftarrow\Rightarrow\,\cdots\,\Leftarrow\Rightarrow{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\!\!\Leftarrow}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Leftarrow}}\Rightarrow\Leftarrow\,\cdots\,\Rightarrow\Leftarrow\,\downarrow\,\rangle\equiv\prod_{j=k+1}^{2N-1}\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j}\,\ket*{\mathrm{\hbox{N\'eel}}^{\mathrm{SPT}}_{x}, -,\uparrow\downarrow},

(90b)

for OBC. These are two excited states
out of the
8(2N-3)
-dimensional eigenspace with eigenenergy
E^{\,}_{8}

that support one domain wall
[compare with Eq. (
54a
)].
Equipped with the ground state (
89b
),
we define for any
k=1,\cdots,2N-1

the pair of orthonormal excited eigenstates

\displaystyle\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{2}}^{\,}_{k}\coloneqq|\,\Rightarrow\,\Leftarrow\Rightarrow\,\cdots\,\Leftarrow{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\!\!\Rightarrow}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Rightarrow\!\!}}\Leftarrow\,\Rightarrow\Leftarrow\,\cdots\,\Rightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!\Leftarrow}}\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Leftarrow}}\,\rangle\equiv\prod_{j=k}^{2N-1}\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j}\,\ket*{\mathrm{\hbox{N\'eel}}^{\mathrm{SPT}}_{x}, -},

(91a)

\displaystyle\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{3}}^{\,}_{k+1}\coloneqq|\,\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Rightarrow}}\Leftarrow\Rightarrow\,\cdots\,\Leftarrow\Rightarrow{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\!\!\Leftarrow}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Leftarrow}}\Rightarrow\Leftarrow\,\cdots\,\Rightarrow\Leftarrow\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\!\!\Rightarrow}}\,\rangle\equiv\prod_{j=k+1}^{2N}\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j}\,\ket*{\mathrm{\hbox{N\'eel}}^{\mathrm{SPT}}_{x}, -},

(91b)

for PBC.
These are two excited states
out of the
2N(2N-1)
-dimensional eigenspace with eigenenergy
E^{\,}_{2}

that support two domain walls
[compare with Eq. (
54b
)].
We define the symmetric and antisymmetric linear combinations

\ket*{+^{\,}_{\mathrm{OBC}}}^{\,}_{k,k+1}\coloneqq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{8}}^{\,}_{k}+\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{9}}^{\,}_{k+1}\right),\qquad\ket*{-^{\,}_{\mathrm{OBC}}}^{\,}_{k,k+1}\coloneqq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{8}}^{\,}_{k}-\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{9}}^{\,}_{k+1}\right),

(92a)

for OBC and

\ket*{+^{\,}_{\mathrm{PBC}}}^{\,}_{k,k+1}\coloneqq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{2}}^{\,}_{k}+\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{3}}^{\,}_{k+1}\right),\qquad\ket*{-^{\,}_{\mathrm{PBC}}}^{\,}_{k,k+1}\coloneqq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{2}}^{\,}_{k}-\ket*{\Psi^{\,}_{3}}^{\,}_{k+1}\right),

(92b)

for PBC.
One verifies that

\displaystyle\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{k}\,\ket*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{OBC}}}^{\,}_{k,k+1}=\pm\ket*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{OBC}}}^{\,}_{k,k+1},

(93a)

\displaystyle\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{k}\,\ket*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{PBC}}}^{\,}_{k,k+1}=\pm\ket*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{PBC}}}^{\,}_{k,k+1},\quad\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{2N}\,\ket*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{PBC}}}^{\,}_{k,k+1}=\pm\ket*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{PBC}}}^{\,}_{k,k+1},

(93b)

for
k=2,\cdots,2N-1
, from which it follows that

\displaystyle{}^{\,}_{k,k+1}\!\bra*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{OBC}}}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{k}\,\ket*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{OBC}}}^{\,}_{k,k+1}=\pm 1,

(94a)

\displaystyle{}^{\,}_{k,k+1}\!\bra*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{PBC}}}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{k}\,\ket*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{PBC}}}^{\,}_{k,k+1}=\pm 1,\qquad{}^{\,}_{k,k+1}\!\bra*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{PBC}}}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{2N}\,\ket*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{PBC}}}^{\,}_{k,k+1}=\pm 1.

(94b)

Moreover, because

\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{k}

and

\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{k-1}\,\widehat{X}^{\,}_{k}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{k+1}

anticommute, the effect of

\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{k-1}\,\widehat{X}^{\,}_{k}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{k+1}

on the eigenstates
of
\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{k}

with eigenvalues differing by a sign
is to interchange them. We then deduce that

\displaystyle{}^{\,}_{k,k+1}\!\bra*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{OBC}}}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{k-1}\,\widehat{X}^{\,}_{k}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{k+1}\,\ket*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{OBC}}}^{\,}_{k,k+1}=0,

(95a)

\displaystyle{}^{\,}_{k,k+1}\!\bra*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{PBC}}}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{k-1}\,\widehat{X}^{\,}_{k}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{k+1}\,\ket*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{PBC}}}^{\,}_{k,k+1}=0,\qquad{}^{\,}_{k,k+1}\!\bra*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{PBC}}}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{2N-1}\,\widehat{X}^{\,}_{2N}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{1}\,\ket*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{PBC}}}^{\,}_{k,k+1}=0.

(95b)

On the other hand, for any
l\neq k,2N
, we have

\displaystyle{}^{\,}_{k,k+1}\!\bra*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{OBC}}}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{l}\,\ket*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{OBC}}}^{\,}_{k,k+1}=0,

(96a)

\displaystyle{}^{\,}_{k,k+1}\!\bra*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{PBC}}}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{l}\,\ket*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{PBC}}}^{\,}_{k,k+1}=0,\qquad{}^{\,}_{k,k+1}\!\bra*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{PBC}}}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{2N}\,\ket*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{PBC}}}^{\,}_{k,k+1}=0,

(96b)

and

\displaystyle{}^{\,}_{k,k+1}\!\bra*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{OBC}}}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{l-1}\,\widehat{X}^{\,}_{l}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{l+1}\,\ket*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{OBC}}}^{\,}_{k,k+1}=(-1)^{\delta^{\,}_{1,\mathrm{sgn}(l-k)}}\,(-1)^{l+1},

(97a)

\displaystyle{}^{\,}_{k,k+1}\!\bra*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{PBC}}}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{l-1}\,\widehat{X}^{\,}_{l}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{l+1}\,\ket*{\pm^{\,}_{\mathrm{PBC}}}^{\,}_{k,k+1}=(-1)^{\delta^{\,}_{1,\mathrm{sgn}(l-k)}}\,(-1)^{l+1}.

(97b)

These expectation values for OBC explain what is observed numerically in
Fig.
14
.

At
(19\pi/20,9/10)
from Fig.
5
,
expectation values in the ground state
|\Psi^{\,}_{0}\rangle

have the following features.
The dependence on
j=2,\cdots,2N-1

of the local cluster expectation value

\langle\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j-1}\,\widehat{X}^{\,}_{j}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j+1}\rangle^{\,}_{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}

is
0.99\times(-1)^{j+1}
,
while

\langle\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j-1}\,\widehat{Y}^{\,}_{j}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j+1}\rangle^{\,}_{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}
,

\langle\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j-1}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j+1}\rangle^{\,}_{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}
,

\langle\widehat{X}^{\,}_{j}\rangle^{\,}_{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}
,

\langle\widehat{Y}^{\,}_{j}\rangle^{\,}_{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}
,
and

\langle\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j}\rangle^{\,}_{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}

all vanish
for both OBC
(b=0)
in Fig.
14

and PBC
(b=1
) in Fig.
15
.
For OBC,

\langle\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{1}\rangle^{\,}_{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}
,
and

\langle\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{32}\rangle^{\,}_{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}

acquire non-vanishing expectation values at the end points

and

, unlike for the PBC case.

At

from Fig.

,
expectation values in the excited state
|\Psi^{\,}_{8}\rangle

have the following features. For PBC,

is

,

and

.

III.1.5
The
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\mathrm{SPT}}_{y}
phase

The
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\mathrm{SPT}}_{y}

phase is the only one of the four gapped phases
for which it is possible to find a ground state
|\Psi\rangle
such that,
in the thermodynamic limit,

\overline{G^{2,x}}[\Psi]=0,\qquad\overline{G^{2,y}}[\Psi]=0,\qquad\overline{G^{4,x}}[\Psi]\neq 0,\qquad\overline{G^{4,y}}[\Psi]=0,

(98)

in Fig.
7
.

The unitary transformation
\widehat{U}^{\,}_{\mathrm{R}}

defined in Eq. (
12
)
realizes the reflection symmetry of
the phase diagram in Fig.
5

with respect to
\theta=\pi/4
,
which holds for both OBC and PBC.
More specifically, the Hamiltonian (
7
)
transforms under conjugation by
\widehat{U}^{\,}_{\mathrm{R}}
as

\widehat{U}^{\,}_{\mathrm{R}}\,\widehat{H}^{\,}_{b}(\theta,\lambda)\,\widehat{U}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{R}}=\widehat{H}^{\,}_{b}(\pi/2-\theta,\lambda),

(99)

for
b=0,1
.
Consequently, the description of the
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\mathrm{SPT}}_{x}
phase
in Sec.
III.1.4

equally holds for
the
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\mathrm{SPT}}_{y}
phase if the following replacements

(\theta,\lambda)\to(\pi/2-\theta,\lambda),\qquad\widehat{X}^{\,}_{j}\to\widehat{Y}^{\,}_{j},\qquad m^{x}_{\mathrm{sta,SPT}}\to m^{y}_{\mathrm{sta,SPT}},

(100)

are made. We have verified numerically the equivalence between
the properties of
the
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\mathrm{SPT}}_{x}

and the
\mathrm{\hbox{N\'{e}el}}^{\mathrm{SPT}}_{y}
phases
once the identifications
(
100
) are performed.

Figure 16:
(Color online)
The case of PBC.
Dependence on the site index
j=1,\cdots,2N
of the local
cluster and spin expectation values obtained
using the DMRG approximation
(
\chi=128
and
2N=32
) to
the ground state
|\Psi^{\,}_{0}\rangle
whose energy is

E^{\,}_{0}
at
(a)
(\pi/4,1/2)
,
(b)
(\pi/4,3/10)
, and
(c)
(\pi/4,7/10)
.

III.1.6
The
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase

According to Fig.
6
,
a fifth phase, centered around the point

(\pi/4,1/2)
, is observed.
This phase has a diamond-like shape and
exhibits a twofold degeneracy for both PBC and OBC.
We label this phase the
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase.
We now explain why this interpretation is appropriate.
We consider first the case of PBC.

As is illustrated in Fig.
16

for PBC,
the local spin expectation value

\expectationvalue*{\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j}}^{\,}_{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}

(
\expectationvalue*{\widehat{X}^{\,}_{j}}^{\,}_{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}
,

\expectationvalue*{\widehat{Y}^{\,}_{j}}^{\,}_{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}
)
along the
Z
-axis (
X
-,
Y
-axis)
in spin space
is non-vanishing (vanishing) and independent of
j=1,\cdots,2N
,
for any point in the
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase
from Fig.
5

with
\Psi^{\,}_{0}
the ground state approximated by DMRG.
For example, we read from Fig.
16
the values

\displaystyle m^{z}_{\mathrm{uni}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]=\begin{cases}0.3125,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4,3/10)$,}\\
0.3750,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4,1/2)$,}\\
0.3125,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4,7/10)$.}\\
\end{cases}

(101)

The magnetization
m^{z}_{\mathrm{uni}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

defined in Eq. (
44a
)
is observed to reach its maximum
\approx 0.4

in absolute value at the center

(\pi/4,1/2)
of Fig.
5
,
whereby, the larger the system size
2N
,
the closer to
0.4
the magnetization

m^{z}_{\mathrm{uni}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]
.
The magnetization
m^{z}_{\mathrm{uni}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

converges to zero upon approaching the boundaries of the

\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase.
The quantum fluctuations are maximum at the
center
(\pi/4,1/2)
of Fig.
5

due to the non-commuting nature
of the competing terms in
Hamiltonian (
7
)
and the enhanced symmetries of
Hamiltonian (
7
) at this point.
We interpret the fact that the
magnetization
m^{z}_{\mathrm{uni}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

peaks in absolute value at
(\pi/4,1/2)

as a manifestation of order by disorder.

As is also illustrated in
Fig.
16

for PBC, the cluster expectation value

\expectationvalue*{\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j-1}\,
\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j}\,
\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j+1}}^{\,}_{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}

(
\expectationvalue*{\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j-1}\,
\widehat{X}^{\,}_{j}\,
\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j+1}}^{\,}_{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}
,

\expectationvalue*{\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j-1}\,
\widehat{Y}^{\,}_{j}\,
\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j+1}}^{\,}_{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}
)
is non-vanishing (vanishing) and independent of
j=1,\cdots,2N
,
for any point in the
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase
from Fig.
5

with
\Psi^{\,}_{0}
the ground state approximated by DMRG.
The uniform SPT magnetization defined in
Eq. (
44c
)
has the opposite sign from that of the uniform magnetization
at any point in the
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase. From
Fig.
16

we read the values

\displaystyle m^{z}_{\mathrm{uni,SPT}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]=\begin{cases}-0.493,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4,3/10)$,}\\
-0.582,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4,1/2)$,}\\
-0.493,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4,7/10)$.}\\
\end{cases}

(102)

In summary, the
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase under PBC
supports long-range order along the

Z
-axis in spin space, with a uniform magnetization
no greater than
40\%
of the uniform magnetization of
the ferromagnetic product state

|\mathrm{Ferro}^{\,}_{z}\rangle\equiv\ket*{\uparrow\ \uparrow\ \cdots\ \uparrow},\qquad\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j}\,|\uparrow\rangle^{\,}_{j}=+|\uparrow\rangle^{\,}_{j},\qquad\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j}\,|\downarrow\rangle^{\,}_{j}=-|\downarrow\rangle^{\,}_{j}.

(103)

The
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase has
two orthonormal ground states

|\Psi^{\,}_{0}\rangle,\qquad|\Psi^{\,}_{1}\rangle=\widehat{T}\,|\Psi^{\,}_{0}\rangle,

(104a)

that support a uniform magnetization
of opposite signs and of magnitude

m^{z}_{\mathrm{uni}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]=-m^{z}_{\mathrm{uni}}[\Psi^{\,}_{1}]\neq 0.

(104b)

It has been shown in Refs.
[
59
,
60
,
61
]

that Hamiltonian (
7
)
at
(\pi/4,1/2)
is Bethe-Ansatz integrable,
realizes a conformal field theory with
central charge
\mathsf{c}=1
,
and supports ferromagnetic long range order.
The ground state energy per site that we have computed numerically
and plotted as a function of system size in
Fig.
17
,
agrees with the prediction for it made with the Bethe Ansatz
in Refs.
[
59
,
60
,
61
]
.
It was also shown in Ref.
[
58
]
that
Hamiltonian (
7
)
at
(\pi/4,1/2)
exhibits Hilbert-space fragmentation.

Figure 17:
(Color online)
The case of PBC.
Ground-state energy
E^{\,}_{0}
per site
as a function of the system size
2N

of Hamiltonian (
7
)
at the point
(\pi/4,1/2)
.

[
59
]
,
provided
E^{\,}_{0}
is substituted with

\sqrt{2}\,E^{\,}_{0}
.
The dashed blue line is the value of

\lim\limits_{2N\uparrow\infty}(E^{\,}_{0}/2N)

computed from the thermodynamic Bethe Ansatz
in Ref.
[
61
]
(i.e.,
-4\sqrt{2}\times 0.2172
).

The case of OBC displays strong boundary effects
which are tied to criticality and incommensuration effects, as we now discuss.

III.2
Criticality and incommensuration of the
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase

Of all five phases in Fig.
5
,
it is only the
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase that has a distinctive coloring in
all panels from
Fig.
7
.
The interpretation of
Figs.
7
,
7
,
7
,
7
,
7
,
and
16

is that the

\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase exhibits
long-range ferromagnetic (FM) order along the
Z
-axis
in spin space.

The fact that
\overline{G^{r,z}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]
,
with
|\Psi^{\,}_{0}\rangle
the
DMRG approximation to the
ground state of Hamiltonian (
7
),
varies and changes sign as a function of

r
strongly suggests
the existence of oscillations in the spin-spin correlations along the

Z
-axis in spin space,
with the period of oscillations
controlled by

\lambda
. This observation aligns with the
empirical fact that
\expectationvalue*{\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j}}^{\,}_{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}

displays oscillations as a function of
j

when OBC are selected, as we shall discuss in section

III.2.2
.

Figures
7
,
7
,
7
, and
7
,
imply the non-vanishing values of

\overline{G^{2,x}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]\neq 0,\qquad\overline{G^{2,y}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]\neq 0,\qquad\overline{G^{4,x}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]\neq 0,\qquad\overline{G^{4,y}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]\neq 0,

(105)

in the
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase.
This empirical fact suggests
that the
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase
supports, in addition to
long-range FM order along the
Z
-axis in spin space,
quasi-long-range AFM
order for the local spins and local clusters
along the
X
- and
Y
-axes in spin space.
We are going to give in what follows supporting evidences for the claim that
long-range FM order coexists with AFM criticality
in the
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase.

(Color online)

s
given
j^{\,}_{0}
for the

),

of Hamiltonian (

)

,

and

and
(b)
under PBC,
at
(\theta,\lambda)=(\pi/4,1/2)
,

,

and

.

“p” for power law,
“e” for exponential,
Goodness of fit for “p” is always greater than that for “e”.

\eta^{x}
and
\eta^{y}

are independent of the choice for
j^{\,}_{0}
.
The insets show the Fourier transforms of the respective

f\equiv q/(2\pi)

and the ordinate is the expansion coefficient
A(f)
.

(d) same as (b) as a log-log plot.

III.2.1
Criticality

The criticality of the
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase
in Fig.
5

is corroborated by three observations.

a
.

First, the two transverse components

C^{\alpha}_{j^{\,}_{0},j^{\,}_{0}+s}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

of the spin-spin correlation functions
in the DMRG approximation to the ground state

|\Psi^{\,}_{0}\rangle

of Hamiltonian (
7
)
defined in Eq. (
41a
)
decay algebraically as

C^{\alpha}_{j^{\,}_{0},j^{\,}_{0}+s}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]\propto(-1)^{s}\,{s}^{-\eta^{\alpha}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]},\qquad 1\ll j^{\,}_{0},s,j^{\,}_{0}+s\ll 2N,\qquad\alpha=x,y,

(106)

with both PBC and OBC for any point
in the
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase from Fig.
5
.

b
.

Second, any point in
the
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase from Fig.
5

realizes a CFT with the central charge
\mathsf{c}=1
.

c
.

Finally, any energy spacing between non-degenerate
consecutive eigenenergies
scales as
1/(2N)
in the thermodynamic limit
for any point in the
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase from Fig.
5
.

Quasi-long-range AFM order
in the
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase from Fig.
5
—

Representative examples of the dependence on the separation
s

of the transverse spin-spin correlation functions
(
41a
)
in the
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase from Fig.
5

are shown in
Figs.
18
and

18

for OBC,
and
Figs.
18
and

18

for PBC.
The best fit selects the algebraic decay
(
106
)
for the transverse components,
whereby

\eta^{x}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]=0.77\pm 0.01,\qquad\eta^{y}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]=1.15\pm 0.04,

(107a)

for
(\theta,\lambda)=(\pi/4-0.1,0.4)
and under OBC,
while

\eta^{x}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]=\eta^{y}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]=1.01\pm 0.04,

(107b)

for
(\theta,\lambda)=(\pi/4,1/2)
and under PBC.

Figure 19:
(Color online)
Dependence on the number
2N
of sites of the
entanglement entropy
S^{\,}_{\mathrm{MPS},2N}({l})

at
(\pi/4,1/2)
.
The entanglement entropy
S^{\,}_{\mathrm{MPS},2N}({l})
is defined in
Eq. (
110
)
for a matrix product state
obtained using DMRG with
(a) OBC and
\chi=256
and
(b) PBC and
\chi=512
.
The slope of the linear fit performed for each system
size
2N
gives
the central charge
\mathsf{c}
in the insets of
each panel. Error bars are given
in the inset of (a).
For all panels, the value of

\delta{l}
defined in Eq. (
111
)
is
2N/2
.

CFT with central charge
\mathsf{c}=1
—

The
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase exhibits quasi-long-range order along the
X
-
and
Y
-axes, suggesting that each point within this phase realizes a
CFT with central charge
\mathsf{c}
.
We have determined
the value of the central charge

\mathsf{c}\coloneqq\lim_{2N\to\infty}\mathsf{c}^{\,}_{2N}

(108a)

in the
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase from Fig.
5

by comparing the CFT prediction

[
68
]

S^{\,}_{\mathrm{CFT},2N}({l})=\begin{cases}\frac{\mathsf{c}^{\,}_{2N}}{3}\,\ln\left(\frac{2N}{\pi}\,\sin\left(\frac{\pi{l}}{2N}\right)\right)+\hbox{ const},&\hbox{ for PBC},\\
&\\
\frac{\mathsf{c}^{\,}_{2N}}{6}\,\ln\left(\frac{4N}{\pi}\,\sin\left(\frac{\pi{l}}{2N}\right)\right)+\hbox{ const},&\hbox{ for OBC},\end{cases}

(108b)

for the bipartite entanglement entropy
S^{\,}_{2N}({l})

against its numerical evaluation
S^{\,}_{\mathrm{MPS},2N}({l})
.
Hereby, we define the entanglement
entropy
S^{\,}_{\mathrm{MPS},2N}({l})

on the bond
{l}
of the matrix product state that
approximates the ground state of Hamiltonian
(
7
) as follows.
The chain of length
2N
is partitioned into the
sites
j=1,\cdots,2N^{\,}_{A}
that define subsystem
A

with the Hilbert space

\mathfrak{H}^{\,}_{A}\cong\mathbb{C}^{2^{4N^{\,}_{A}}}=\mathrm{span}\left\{|\mathfrak{b}^{\,}_{A}\rangle\ \Big|\ \langle\mathfrak{b}^{\,}_{A}|\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}_{A}\rangle=\delta^{\,}_{\mathfrak{b}^{\,}_{A},\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}_{A}},\qquad\mathfrak{b}^{\,}_{A},\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}_{A}=1,\cdots,2^{4N^{\,}_{A}}\right\}

(109a)

and the sites
2N^{\,}_{A}+1,\cdots,2N
that define subsystem
B

with the Hilbert space

\mathfrak{H}^{\,}_{B}\cong\mathbb{C}^{2^{4N-4N^{\,}_{A}}}=\mathrm{span}\left\{|\mathfrak{b}^{\,}_{B}\rangle\ \Big|\ \langle\mathfrak{b}^{\,}_{B}|\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}_{B}\rangle=\delta^{\,}_{\mathfrak{b}^{\,}_{B},\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}_{B}},\qquad\mathfrak{b}^{\,}_{B},\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}_{B}=1,\cdots,2^{4N-4N^{\,}_{A}}\right\}.

(109b)

Any state
|\Psi\rangle\in\mathbb{C}^{2^{4N}}

can be expanded in the
tensor basis of
\mathfrak{H}^{\,}_{A}\otimes\mathfrak{H}^{\,}_{B}
,

|\Psi\rangle=\sum_{\mathfrak{b}^{\,}_{A}=1}^{2^{4N^{\,}_{A}}}\sum_{\mathfrak{b}^{\,}_{B}=1}^{2^{4N-4N^{\,}_{A}}}M^{\,}_{\mathfrak{b}^{\,}_{A},\mathfrak{b}^{\,}_{B}}\,|\mathfrak{b}^{\,}_{A}\rangle\otimes|\mathfrak{b}^{\,}_{B}\rangle,\qquad M^{\,}_{\mathfrak{b}^{\,}_{A},\mathfrak{b}^{\,}_{B}}=M^{\,}_{\mathfrak{b}^{\,}_{B},\mathfrak{b}^{\,}_{A}}\in\mathbb{C}.

(109c)

If we denote with
s
the singular values of
the matrix with the matrix elements

M^{\,}_{\mathfrak{b}^{\,}_{A},\mathfrak{b}^{\,}_{B}}
,
then the bipartite von Neumann entropy of the state
|\Psi\rangle

is defined by

S^{\,}_{N^{\,}_{A},N^{\,}_{B}}[\psi]\coloneqq-\sum_{s}s^{2}\ln s^{2}.

(109d)

For our matrix product state obtained from DMRG,
assuming that it is
in the so-called mixed canonical form on the bond

{l}
with
1<{l}<2N
and bond dimension
\chi
,
its entanglement entropy is

S^{\,}_{\mathrm{MPS},2N}({l})=-\sum_{i=1}^{\chi}s^{2}_{i}({l})\,\ln s^{2}_{i}({l}),

(110)

where
s^{\,}_{i}({l})
is the
i^{\mathrm{th}}
singular value
associated to a bipartition of cardinality
{l}\,\equiv 2N^{\,}_{A}
for subsystem
A

and cardinality
2N-{l}
for subsystem
B
.

We have used DMRG to evaluate
the bipartite entanglement entropy

S^{\,}_{2N}(l)

in the
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase of Fig.
5
.
By plotting the dependence of

S^{\,}_{\mathrm{MPS},2N}(l)

on

\ln\left(\frac{4N}{\pi}\,\sin\left(\frac{\pi{l}}{2N}\right)\right)
,
we were able to confirm the validity of
the CFT prediction
(
108
)
with
\mathsf{c}=1
.
A representative example of such a procedure
at the center
(\pi/4,1/2)

of the phase diagram

5

is found in Fig.
19
.
The range over which

S^{\,}_{\mathrm{MPS},2N}({l})

defined in Eq. (
110
)
is fitted by

S^{\,}_{\mathrm{CFT},2N}({l})

defined in Eq. (
108b
)
is

\delta{l}\equiv{l}^{\,}_{\mathrm{max}}-{l}^{\,}_{\mathrm{min}},\qquad 1\leq{l}^{\,}_{\mathrm{min}}<{l}^{\,}_{\mathrm{max}}\leq 2N.

(111a)

Here, we choose

\delta{l}\approx 2N/2,\qquad{l}^{\,}_{\mathrm{min}}\approx 2N/4,\qquad 2N-{l}^{\,}_{\mathrm{max}}\approx 2N/4,

(111b)

so that the bonds
{l}^{\,}_{\mathrm{min}}
and
{l}^{\,}_{\mathrm{max}}

are each a distance of
2N/4
away from
the first bond
{l}=1
and the last bond
{l}=2N-1

entering the matrix product state. The range
\delta{l}
must be a
finite fraction of the number
2N
of sites because the initial
matrix product state used for DMRG under both PBC and OBC
breaks explicitly the translation symmetry.

In Fig.
19
, the bipartite entanglement entropy

S^{\,}_{\mathrm{MPS},2N}({l})

shows oscillations as a function of
{l}
. This oscillatory behaviour is a the
signature of the incommensuration in the
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase
[
70
]
, which we shall explore in Sec.
III.2.2
.
It is well known that extracting the central charge in finite systems
is more accurate with PBC, as finite-size effects are strongly
suppressed compared to OBC.
However, choosing PBC significantly
increases the computational cost of the DMRG algorithm
[
63
]

10
10

10

Specifically, if DMRG converges to a ground state with variance
V

using a bond dimension
\chi
with OBC, achieving the same variance

V
with PBC requires a bond dimension of
\chi^{2}
. This increases
the computational cost of DMRG by
\mathcal{O}(\chi^{3})
when using
PBC over OBC
.
Imposing PBC, we find that
(\pi/4,1/2)
realizes a CFT with

\mathsf{c}=1
. We computed
\mathsf{c}
for other points inside
the
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase and consistently found
\mathsf{c}=1
with the
same level of precision as in Fig.
19
.

of the

.

(

).

with

,

to the colors of the

Gapless excitations —

An alternative strategy in support of the existence
of a CFT that encodes criticality
for any point in the
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase from Fig.
5

involves analyzing the dependence on the system size
2N

in the limit
2N\to\infty

of the differences

\delta^{\,}_{n}(2N)\coloneqq E^{\,}_{n}(2N)-E^{\,}_{n-1}(2N),\qquad n=1,2,\cdots,

(112)

between consecutive low-lying energy eigenvalues.
For these low-lying excited energy eigenvalues

11
11

11

Only the low lying excitations
associated to the primary fields
(as opposed to their descendants, say)
are considered.

,
CFT implies the dependence on the length
L\equiv 2N\,\mathfrak{a}

of the chain with the lattice spacing
\mathfrak{a}
given by

E^{\,}_{n}(L)-E^{\,}_{0}(L)=\sum_{m=1}^{n}\delta^{\,}_{m}(L)\sim\frac{2\pi}{L}\,\hbar\,v\,\Delta^{\,}_{n}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{L\,\ln L}\right),

(113)

where
E^{\,}_{n}(L)
is the
n^{\mathrm{th}}

excited energy above the ground-state energy

E^{\,}_{0}(L)
when PBC are selected,

v
plays the role of the “speed of light” in the CFT,
and
\Delta^{\,}_{n}
denotes the scaling dimension
of the primary fields

\phi^{\,}_{n}

in the CFT
that is associated to the eigenenergy
E^{\,}_{n}

[
77
]
,
i.e.,
the scaling dimension
\Delta^{\,}_{n}
is the power that characterizes
the algebraic decay of the two-point function for
the primary field
\phi^{\,}_{n}
. Numerical evaluation of
the dependence on
1/L
of Eq. (
113
)
and of the dependence on the separation
s
entering the two-point
function for the primary field
\phi^{\,}_{n}
allows
to extract the “speed of light”
v

and the scaling dimension
\Delta^{\,}_{n}
.
The central charge
\mathsf{c}
of the CFT can then be
determined by the numerical estimate of the dependence on
L

of the ground-state energy

E^{\,}_{0}(L)\sim e^{\,}_{0}\times L-\frac{\pi\,\hbar\,v\,\mathsf{c}}{6\,L}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{L\,\ln L}\right),

(114)

where
e^{\,}_{0}

is the ground-state energy per site
in the thermodynamic limit
L\equiv 2N\,\mathfrak{a}\to\infty

[
77
]
.
However, an accurate estimate
for the “speed of light”

v
proved challenging.

Indeed, we were unable to measure the product

v\,\Delta^{\,}_{n}

on the right-hand side of Eq. (
113
),
because we found that the dependence on
2N
of the energy difference
(
112
)
is of the form

\delta^{\,}_{n}(2N)\sim\frac{\cos(Q^{\,}_{n}\times 2N)}{2N},\qquad Q^{\,}_{n}\approx\frac{2\pi}{10},

(115)

in the
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase from Fig.
5
,
as is illustrated in Fig.
20
.
Figure
20
applies to
the center
(\pi/4,1/2)
of Fig.
5
.
Figure
20

shows that the dependence on the number
2N
of lattice points
of the energy difference
E^{\,}_{4}-E^{\,}_{3}

takes the same form as the right-hand side of
Eq. (
115
).
A periodic oscillation is superimposed over the power law
decay
1/(2N)
.
Figures
20

and
20

for
E^{\,}_{2m+1}-E^{\,}_{2m}
with
m=0,1,2

show that
E^{\,}_{2m+1}-E^{\,}_{2m}
is exponentially suppressed
as a function of
2N
, i.e., that

E^{\,}_{2m+1}
and
E^{\,}_{2m}
become degenerate in the thermodynamic limit.
Figures
20

and
20

for
E^{\,}_{2m}-E^{\,}_{2m-1}
with
m=1,2
,
show that
E^{\,}_{2m}-E^{\,}_{2m-1}

is of the form (
115
)
with a characteristic periodicity of order
2\pi\,Q^{-1}_{n}\approx 10

that prevents access to sufficiently large systems sizes
for an accurate estimate of

v\,\Delta^{\,}_{n}
.

(Color online)

of the local

(
\chi=128
and

)
to the ground state
|\Psi^{\,}_{0}\rangle
whose energy is

at
(a)
(\pi/4,1/2)
,

,

, and

(\pi/4-0.1,1/2)

from Fig.

.
Dependence on
j=1,\cdots,2N
of the local
spin expectations values computed using the DMRG approximation
(
\chi=128
and
2N=256
)
to the ground state
|\Psi^{\,}_{0}\rangle

whose energy is
E^{\,}_{0}
at
(e)
(\pi/4,1/2)
and
(f)
(\pi/4,7/10)

from Fig.
5
.

III.2.2
Incommensuration

Incommensuration effects become manifest upon using OBC as opposed
to PBC. For example, the non-vanishing local expectation values
from Fig.
16
are independent of the site index
when PBC are selected, while they become strongly site-dependent
when OBC are selected as is
illustrated in Fig.
21
.

According to Fig.
21
,
the only non-vanishing local expectation values are
the ground-state expectation values of

\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j}

and

\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j-1}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j+1}
.
For a chain made of
2N=32
sites under OBC,
they oscillate around the mean values

\displaystyle m^{z}_{\mathrm{uni}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]=\begin{cases}0.373,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4-0.1,1/2)$},\\
0.375,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4,7/10)$},\\
0.375,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4,1/2)$},\\
0.312,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4,3/10)$},\\
0.373,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4+0.1,1/2)$},\end{cases}

(116a)

defined in Eq. (
44a
)
and

\displaystyle m^{z}_{\mathrm{uni},\mathrm{SPT}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]=\begin{cases}-0.591,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4-0.1,1/2)$},\\
-0.543,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4,7/10)$},\\
-0.594,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4,1/2)$},\\
-0.507,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4,3/10)$},\\
-0.591,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4+0.1,1/2)$},\end{cases}

(116b)

defined in Eq. (
44c
).
As was the case with
Eq. (
101
),
these mean values
reach their maxima in absolute value at the center

(\pi/4,1/2)
of the phase diagram
5
.
As was the case with
Eq. (
101
),
they are symmetric with respect to the mirror symmetry about the

\mathrm{U}(1)
-symmetry axis
\theta=\pi/4
.
Unlike was the case with
Eq. (
101
),
they are not symmetric
about the axis
\lambda=1/2
.
For a chain of length
2N=32
,
the ground-state expectation values of

\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j}

and

\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j-1}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j+1}

can be fitted with the pair of Ansaetze

\langle\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j}\rangle^{\,}_{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}=m^{z}_{\mathrm{uni}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]+A^{z}_{\mathrm{loc}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]\,\cos\big(
q^{z}_{\mathrm{loc}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]\,j
+
\phi^{z}_{\mathrm{loc}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]
\big),\qquad 1\ll j\ll 2N,\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad

(117a)

and

\langle\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j-1}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j+1}\rangle^{\,}_{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}=m^{z}_{\mathrm{uni,SPT}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]+A^{z}_{\mathrm{loc,SPT}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]\,\cos\big(
q^{z}_{\mathrm{loc,SPT}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]\,j
+
\phi^{z}_{\mathrm{loc,SPT}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]
\big),\qquad 1\ll j\ll 2N,

(117b)

respectively,
whereby the wave numbers of the oscillations are

\displaystyle\frac{q^{z}_{\mathrm{loc}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]}{2\pi}=\begin{cases}0.313,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4-0.1,7/10)$},\\
0.324,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4,7/10)$},\\
0.313,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4,1/2)$},\\
0.323,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4,3/10)$},\\
0.313,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4+0.1,7/10)$},\end{cases}

(118a)

for

\langle\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j}\rangle^{\,}_{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}

and

\displaystyle\frac{q^{z}_{\mathrm{loc,SPT}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]}{2\pi}=\begin{cases}0.313,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4-0.1,7/10)$},\\
0.324,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4,7/10)$},\\
0.313,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4,1/2)$},\\
0.325,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4,3/10)$},\\
0.313,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4+0.1,7/10)$},\end{cases}

(118b)

for

\langle\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j-1}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j}\,\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j+1}\rangle^{\,}_{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}
.
We empirically observe that

q^{z}_{\mathrm{loc}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

and

q^{z}_{\mathrm{loc,SPT}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

are a function of
|1/2-\lambda|
only.

To obtain a more precise estimate of

m^{z}_{\mathrm{uni}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

and
q^{z}_{\mathrm{loc}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]
,
we compute these quantities on a chain
of length
2N=256
[see Figs.
21

and
21
]. The local spin expectation
values
\expectationvalue*{\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j}}^{\,}_{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}

oscillates around the mean values

\displaystyle m^{z}_{\mathrm{uni}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]=\begin{cases}0.340,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4,7/10)$},\\
0.398,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4,1/2)$},\\
0.337,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4,3/10)$},\end{cases}

(119a)

with the wave number of oscillations

\displaystyle\frac{q^{z}_{\mathrm{loc}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]}{2\pi}=\begin{cases}0.323,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4,7/10)$},\\
0.301,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4,1/2)$},\\
0.328,&\hbox{ at $(\pi/4,3/10)$}.\end{cases}

(119b)

We observe in
Eq. (
119a
)
that the values of the uniform magnetization

m^{z}_{\mathrm{uni}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

for a chain of
2N=256
sites
is approximately equal at the points

(\pi/4,3/10)
and
(\pi/4,7/10)

as opposed to
Eq. (
116a
)
for a chain of
2N=32
sites.
This observation is explained as follows.
(i) The unitary transformation

\widehat{U}^{\,}_{\mathrm{E}}

defined in Eq. (
12
)
does not realize the reflection symmetry about
the line
\lambda=1/2
under OBC.
(ii) The difference in
m^{z}_{\mathrm{uni}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

at the points

(\pi/4,3/10)
and
(\pi/4,7/10)

is attributed to the effect of the
free spin-
1/2
degrees of freedom
at both ends of the chain.
(iii) The difference in
m^{z}_{\mathrm{uni}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

at the points

(\pi/4,3/10)
and
(\pi/4,7/10)

vanishes in the thermodynamic limit

2N\to\infty
.

The oscillatory behavior observed in
Fig.
21

is a signature of both the incommensurability present in the

\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase in Fig.
5
,
and criticality as

m^{z}_{\mathrm{uni}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

and

m^{z}_{\mathrm{uni,SPT}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

are observed to be sensitive to the choice between PBC and OBC
in the thermodynamic limit.
We now analyze the two-point correlation functions to establish
quantitatively the incommensuration of the
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase,
illustrated in Figs.
22

and
23
for OBC,
and in Fig.
24
for PBC.

Figure 22:
(Color online)
The case of OBC.
Dependence on
s
given
j^{\,}_{0}
for the
spin-spin correlation functions

C^{\alpha}_{j^{\,}_{0},j^{\,}_{0}+s}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

defined in Eq. (
41a
)
and computed using the DMRG approximation (
\chi=128
and
2N=128
)
to the ground state

|\Psi^{\,}_{0}\rangle

of Hamiltonian (
7
)
at
(\theta,\lambda)=(\pi/4-0.1,0.4)
.
(a)

C^{z}_{j^{\,}_{0},j^{\,}_{0}+s}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

for

j^{\,}_{0}=(2N-80)/2
.
(b)

C^{z}_{j^{\,}_{0},j^{\,}_{0}+s}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

for

j^{\,}_{0}=(2N-72)/2
.
Fit used is “q” for cosine form.
In both cases,

q^{z}\approx 2\pi\,\frac{3}{10}
.
The best fit for
q^{z}

is independent of the choice for
j^{\,}_{0}
.
The insets show the Fourier transforms of the respective
correlations, where the abscissa is the frequency

f^{z}\equiv q^{z}/(2\pi)

and the ordinate is the expansion coefficient
A(f^{z})
.

Figure 23:
(Color online)
The case of OBC.
Dependence on the separation
s

of the spin-spin correlation function

C^{z}_{j^{\,}_{0},j^{\,}_{0}+s}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

defined in Eq. (
41a
)
in the ground state
|\Psi^{\,}_{0}\rangle

of Hamiltonian (
7
)
at the center
(\pi/4,1/2)

of the
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase from Fig.
5
.
The ground state is approximated by the DMRG (
\chi=256
)
matrix product state, with
(a)
2N=512
and

j^{\,}_{0}=(2N-256)/2
given and
(b)
2N=1024
and

j^{\,}_{0}=(2N-512)/2
given.
Connected part

C^{z,\mathrm{c}}_{j^{\,}_{0},j^{\,}_{0}+s}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

defined in Eq. (
41b
) with
(a)
2N=512
and

j^{\,}_{0}=(2N-256)/2
given and
(b)
2N=1024
and

j^{\,}_{0}=(2N-512)/2
given.
Fits used are labeled “q”
for cosine form and “d” for an algebraically damped cosine.
In all cases,
q^{z}\simeq 2\pi\,\frac{3}{10}
.
The insets show the Fourier transforms of the respective correlations,
where the abscissa is the frequency
f^{z}\equiv q^{z}/(2\pi)

and the ordinate is the expansion coefficient
A(f^{z})
.

The longitudinal component

C^{z}_{j^{\,}_{0},j^{\,}_{0}+s}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

of the spin-spin correlation function
in the DMRG approximation to the ground state

|\Psi^{\,}_{0}\rangle

of Hamiltonian (
7
)
defined in Eq. (
41a
)
can be fitted by the periodic Ansatz

C^{z}_{j^{\,}_{0},j^{\,}_{0}+s}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]=\Gamma^{z}_{j^{\,}_{0}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]+A^{z}_{j^{\,}_{0}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]\,\cos\big(
q^{z}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]\,s
+
\phi^{z}_{j^{\,}_{0}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]
\big),\qquad 1\ll j^{\,}_{0},s,j^{\,}_{0}+s\ll 2N,

(120)

with the non-vanishing constant

\Gamma^{z}_{j^{\,}_{0}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]
,
the amplitude
A^{z}_{j^{\,}_{0}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]
,
the wave number
q^{z}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

and the phase
\phi^{z}_{j^{\,}_{0}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

real valued.
Under PBC, on the one hand, translation symmetry dictates that

\Gamma^{z}_{j^{\,}_{0}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]
,

A^{z}_{j^{\,}_{0}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]
,

q^{z}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

and
\phi^{z}_{j^{\,}_{0}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

are independent of
j^{\,}_{0}

unless translation symmetry is spontaneously broken.
We observe that there is no dependence on
j^{\,}_{0}

for any of the terms on the right-hand side of
Eq. (
120
)
under PBC. Under OBC, on the other hand, we have anticipated that

q^{z}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

is the only quantity
on the right-hand side of
Eq. (
120
)
that remains independent of
j^{\,}_{0}
in the thermodynamic limit.
This educated guess is confirmed by our numerics.

The best fit to the DMRG data selects the Ansatz
(
120
)
for the longitudinal component of the spin-spin correlation functions
at
(\theta,\lambda)=(\pi/4-0.1,0.4)
,
whereby we read from Fig.
22

\Gamma^{z}_{j^{\,}_{0}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]=0.1653\pm 0.0003,\qquad q^{z}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]=2\pi\left(\frac{3}{10}+0.005\pm 9\times 10^{-5}\right),

(121a)

for
j^{\,}_{0}=(128-80)/2
,
while we read from Fig.
22

\Gamma^{z}_{j^{\,}_{0}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]=0.1183\pm 0.0002,\qquad q^{z}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]=2\pi\left(\frac{3}{10}+0.006\pm 14\times 10^{-5}\right),

(121b)

for
j^{\,}_{0}=(128-72)/2
.
We do not quote the value taken by
A^{z}_{j^{\,}_{0}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

in Eq. (
120
)
as it depends both on the choice of boundary
conditions and on the choice of
j^{\,}_{0}
.
We notice that the value taken by

\Gamma^{z}_{j^{\,}_{0}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

for the choice we made of
j^{\,}_{0}

does not equal the square of the uniform magnetization

m^{z}_{\mathrm{uni}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

in Eq. (
44a
), that we found
in Fig.
16

to be approximately
0.4^{2}=0.16
.
This is a consequence of the observation that
the dependence on
j^{\,}_{0}
of

\Gamma^{z}_{j^{\,}_{0}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

also displays a periodicity [of approximately 10 lattice spacings
at
(\pi/4,1/2)
]. Upon averaging
\Gamma^{z}_{j^{\,}_{0}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

over
j^{\,}_{0}
to account for this periodicity,
we recover the anticipated relation according to
which this average is the square of
m^{z}_{\mathrm{uni}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]
.

The insets in each of the panels in Fig.
22

represent the discrete Fourier transforms of the
correlations. We establish with confidence that the oscillations in

C^{z}_{j^{\,}_{0},j^{\,}_{0}+s}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]
are modulated by a
wave-vector
q^{z}\simeq 2\pi\times 3/10
. However, the discrete
nature of the Fourier transforms limits the precision of
q^{z}
due
to the discretization of the wave-vector interval
[-\pi,\pi]
into

n
values, where
n
is the size of the range of separations
s
used
for the fit. For example,
n=60
in Fig.
22
.
To mitigate this limitation, larger system sizes are
analyzed in Fig.
23
.

In Fig.
23
,
we show the correlations

C^{z}_{j^{\,}_{0},j^{\,}_{0}+s}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

and

C^{z,\mathrm{c}}_{j^{\,}_{0},j^{\,}_{0}+s}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]
, defined
in (
41a
) and (
41b
), respectively,
for larger system sizes
2N=512
and
2N=1024
, at the center

(\pi/4,1/2)
of the
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase.
This allows us to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{q^{z}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]}{2\pi}=\begin{cases}0.30116\pm 1\cdot 10^{-5},&\hbox{ for $2N=512$},\\
0.300804\pm 3\cdot 10^{-6},&\hbox{ for $2N=1024$},\end{cases}

(122)

as shown in Figs.
23

and
23
, respectively, using
the Ansatz
(
120
)
for the two-point correlation function

C^{z}_{j^{\,}_{0},j^{\,}_{0}+s}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]
.

To decide if the oscillations in

C^{z}_{j^{\,}_{0},j^{\,}_{0}+s}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]
originate from the
oscillations in
\expectationvalue*{\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j}}^{\,}_{\Psi^{\,}_{0}}
,
we compute the second cumulant of the operator
\widehat{Z}^{\,}_{j}
,
i.e., the connected correlations

C^{z,\mathrm{c}}_{j^{\,}_{0},j^{\,}_{0}+s}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]
defined in
(
41b
).
Figures
23

and
23
show

C^{z,\mathrm{c}}_{j^{\,}_{0},j^{\,}_{0}+s}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]
for

2N=512
and
2N=1024
, respectively.

In addition to the oscillations that persist, we observe a decay as a
function of
s
. The fit
{}^{\,}_{\mathrm{d}}
that we use is given by

C^{z,\mathrm{c}}_{j^{\,}_{0},j^{\,}_{0}+s}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]=\Gamma^{z,\mathrm{c}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]+A^{z,\mathrm{c}}_{j^{\,}_{0}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]\,s^{-\eta^{z,\mathrm{c}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]}\,\cos\big(q^{z,\mathrm{c}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]\,s
+\phi^{z,\mathrm{c}}_{j^{\,}_{0}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]\big),\qquad 1\ll j^{\,}_{0},s,j^{\,}_{0}+s\ll 2N,

(123)

where only
A^{z,\mathrm{c}}_{j^{\,}_{0}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]
and

\phi^{z,\mathrm{c}}_{j^{\,}_{0}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]
depend on
j^{\,}_{0}
.
We find the exponent of the algebraic decay to take the values

\eta^{z,\mathrm{c}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]=\begin{cases}1.026\pm 0.006,&\hbox{ for $2N=512$},\\
1.002\pm 0.002,&\hbox{ for $2N=1024$},\end{cases}

(124)

at the center
(\pi/4,1/2)
of the
\mathrm{FM}^{\,}_{z}
phase.
The wave-vector
q^{z,\mathrm{c}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]
obtained for

C^{z,\mathrm{c}}_{j^{\,}_{0},j^{\,}_{0}+s}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

lies within the uncertainty interval of the wave-vector

q^{z}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]
obtained for

C^{z}_{j^{\,}_{0},j^{\,}_{0}+s}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

in Eq. (
122
).
We thus claim that
C^{z,\mathrm{c}}_{j^{\,}_{0},j^{\,}_{0}+s}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]

is an algebraically decaying function of the form

s^{-\eta^{z,\mathrm{c}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]}

with exponent
\eta^{z,\mathrm{c}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]=1
,
modulated by oscillations as a function of
s

with wave-vector
q^{z,\mathrm{c}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]=2\pi\times 3/10
,
i.e.,

q^{z}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}]=q^{z,\mathrm{c}}[\Psi^{\,}_{0}].

(125)

of the spin-spin correlation function

)

and

) approximation
to the ground state

of Hamiltonian (

)
at

.

of

for a DMRG with
\chi=512
and

.
Fits used are labeled
“q” for cosine form and

.
